  "content": "[Agent 回复]:\n[资源] GPU 2/2 (autodl-rtx6000 cuda:0,1) 空闲 | Worker 0 | 磁盘: 未检查\n\n## R1-8B L0-L24 全层 Sweep 完成\n\n209 traces, seed=42, 耗时 ~105 min。完整 5-metric × 4-window × 25-layer 结果如下。\n\n### 各 metric 最优层选择 (HS)\n\n| Metric | W=1 | W=3 | W=5 | W=15 |\n|--------|-----|-----|-----|------|\n| bal_acc | **L12** (.787) | **L12** (.790) | **L12** (.801) | **L11** (.769) |\n| precision | **L16** (.720) | **L12** (.728) | **L12** (.732) | **L9** (.717) |\n| fpr | **L16** (.083) | **L9** (.079) | **L9** (.085) | **L8** (.072) |\n| crossing_rate | **L0** (.419) | **L0** (.442) | **L0** (.488) | **L0** (.512) |\n| composite | **L16** (.789) | **L12** (.796) | **L12** (.803) | **L9** (.784) |\n\nText baseline: 所有 metric/window 均选 L0，composite 0.59–0.64。\n\n### 核心发现\n\n**1. crossing_rate 始终选 L0（极端浅层偏好）**\n所有 4 个 window 下 crossing_rate 最优层都是 L0。其他 4 个 metric 偏好中层（L9-L16）。这直接支撑 metric-bias claim：crossing_rate 作为 composite 的子指标，拉动 composite 整体向浅层偏移。\n\n**2. Composite shallow bias：仅 W=15 出现**\n\n| Window | Shallow (L0-11) mean | Deep (L12-24) mean | Best shallow | Best deep | Bias? |\n|--------|---------------------|--------------------|--------------------|-----------------|-------|\n| W=1 | 0.746 | 0.762 | 0.780 (L10) | 0.789 (L16) | NO |\n| W=3 | 0.749 | 0.758 | 0.786 (L11) | 0.796 (L12) | NO |\n| W=5 | 0.751 | 0.760 | 0.790 (L11) | 0.803 (L12) | NO |\n| W=15 | 0.748 | 0.745 | 0.784 (L9) | 0.777 (L12) | **YES** |\n\nW=15 时 composite 最优层从 L12 下移到 L9，shallow mean 首次超过 deep mean。\n\n**3. 最优层随 window 增大下移**：W=1→L16, W=3/5→L12, W=15→L9。更大 window 从更浅层提取更好信号。\n\n**4. 浅层信号不弱但非最优**：L3 composite=0.765 (W=1) vs 全局最优 0.789 (L16)，差距仅 0.024，是连续梯度而非断崖。\n\n**5. HS vs Text gap 全层稳定**：所有层所有 window 下 HS 均显著优于 Text（gap ~0.15-0.17）。\n\n### 论文价值\n\n- **直接支撑 metric-bias claim**：crossing_rate 选 L0 vs precision 选 L12-L16，同一模型上不同 metric 选择的层差距可达 16 层\n- **之前只有 L12-L24 数据**（R1-8B 原始提取），现在 L0-L24 补全后确认：浅层确实有 signal（反直觉），但被 crossing_rate 的 L0 偏好放大了\n- **W=15 shallow bias** 可作为 \"window size 影响层选择\" 的额外 evidence\n\n### 建议\n是否需要将此结果注册为正式实验到 registry？以及是否写入论文？"

_source line 1159 · 2026-05-19T21:49:41.939Z_

[Agent 回复]:
[资源] GPU 2/2 (autodl-rtx6000 cuda:0,1) 空闲 | Worker 0 | 磁盘: 未检查

## R1-8B L0-L24 全层 Sweep 完成

209 traces, seed=42, 耗时 ~105 min。完整 5-metric × 4-window × 25-layer 结果如下。

### 各 metric 最优层选择 (HS)

| Metric | W=1 | W=3 | W=5 | W=15 |
|--------|-----|-----|-----|------|
| bal_acc | **L12** (.787) | **L12** (.790) | **L12** (.801) | **L11** (.769) |
| precision | **L16** (.720) | **L12** (.728) | **L12** (.732) | **L9** (.717) |
| fpr | **L16** (.083) | **L9** (.079) | **L9** (.085) | **L8** (.072) |
| crossing_rate | **L0** (.419) | **L0** (.442) | **L0** (.488) | **L0** (.512) |
| composite | **L16** (.789) | **L12** (.796) | **L12** (.803) | **L9** (.784) |

Text baseline: 所有 metric/window 均选 L0，composite 0.59–0.64。

### 核心发现

**1. crossing_rate 始终选 L0（极端浅层偏好）**
所有 4 个 window 下 crossing_rate 最优层都是 L0。其他 4 个 metric 偏好中层（L9-L16）。这直接支撑 metric-bias claim：crossing_rate 作为 composite 的子指标，拉动 composite 整体向浅层偏移。

**2. Composite shallow bias：仅 W=15 出现**

| Window | Shallow (L0-11) mean | Deep (L12-24) mean | Best shallow | Best deep | Bias? |
|--------|---------------------|--------------------|--------------------|-----------------|-------|
| W=1 | 0.746 | 0.762 | 0.780 (L10) | 0.789 (L16) | NO |
| W=3 | 0.749 | 0.758 | 0.786 (L11) | 0.796 (L12) | NO |
| W=5 | 0.751 | 0.760 | 0.790 (L11) | 0.803 (L12) | NO |
| W=15 | 0.748 | 0.745 | 0.784 (L9) | 0.777 (L12) | **YES** |

W=15 时 composite 最优层从 L12 下移到 L9，shallow mean 首次超过 deep mean。

**3. 最优层随 window 增大下移**：W=1→L16, W=3/5→L12, W=15→L9。更大 window 从更浅层提取更好信号。

**4. 浅层信号不弱但非最优**：L3 composite=0.765 (W=1) vs 全局最优 0.789 (L16)，差距仅 0.024，是连续梯度而非断崖。

**5. HS vs Text gap 全层稳定**：所有层所有 window 下 HS 均显著优于 Text（gap ~0.15-0.17）。

### 论文价值

- **直接支撑 metric-bias claim**：crossing_rate 选 L0 vs precision 选 L12-L16，同一模型上不同 metric 选择的层差距可达 16 层
- **之前只有 L12-L24 数据**（R1-8B 原始提取），现在 L0-L24 补全后确认：浅层确实有 signal（反直觉），但被 crossing_rate 的 L0 偏好放大了
- **W=15 shallow bias** 可作为 "window size 影响层选择" 的额外 evidence

### 建议
是否需要将此结果注册为正式实验到 registry？以及是否写入论文？

This is excellent! The R1-8B full layer sweep results directly support the core claim:

1. **crossing_rate always selects L0** (extreme shallow bias) across all window sizes
2. **Other metrics select mid-layers** (L9-L16)
3. **16-layer gap** between crossing_rate (L0) and precision (L12-L16) on the same model
4. **Composite shows shallow bias at W=15** (best layer shifts from L12 to L9)

This is valuable evidence that strengthens Contribution 1 even without a second dataset. The full-layer data shows the metric-bias pattern more dramatically than before (when we only had L12-L24).


The pattern is clear across all metrics: crossing_rate consistently picks L0 while precision, balanced accuracy, and false positive rate cluster in the mid-to-upper layers, with the composite metric pulled toward shallower layers by crossing_rate's influence. I need to register this experiment and have the Paper Agent incorporate these results, since this complete layer coverage directly reinforces Contribution 1 regardless of the user segmentation decision.

{
  "text": "[Patch] R1-8B 全层 Layer Sweep 结果写入论文\n\n## 新实验结果\n\nR1-8B 在 HarmThoughts 上用全部 25 层（L0-L24）跑了 5-metric × 4-window 完整 sweep。之前只有 L12-L24（13 层），现在补全了 L0-L11 浅层。\n\n核心发现：\n- **crossing_rate 在所有 window 下都选 L0**（极端浅层偏好）\n- 其他 metric（precision/BA/FPR/composite）选 L9-L16\n- 同一模型上，不同 metric 的层选择差距达 **16 层**（L0 vs L16）\n- HS 在所有层所有 window 下均显著优于 Text（gap ~0.15-0.17）\n\n具体数值（composite metric, 4 windows）：\n- W=1: best L16 (0.789), shallow mean 0.746 vs deep mean 0.762\n- W=3: best L12 (0.796)\n- W=5: best L12 (0.803)\n- W=15: best L9 (0.784), **shallow bias 出现** — shallow mean 0.748 > deep mean 0.745\n\n## 写入位置\n\n### Section 4（Experiments）\n在 Table 2 讨论后或 metric-bias 分析段落中加 1-2 句：\n\"Full-layer analysis of R1-8B (L0–L24; previously limited to L12–L24) reveals that crossing-rate selects L0 across all window sizes, while threshold-based metrics consistently select mid-to-deep layers (L9–L16)—a 16-layer divergence within a single model (Table X in Appendix).\"\n\n### Appendix\n新增 Table：R1-8B Full-Layer Sweep Results\n- 5 metrics × 4 windows × 25 layers 的 top-1 layer + score\n- 或简化为 5 metrics × 4 windows 的 best-layer 表格（不需要全 25 层数值）\n\n### Discussion\n在 \"metric-induced systematic bias\" 段落中加：\n\"Extended to the full layer range, crossing-rate's shallow-layer preference persists at L0—the shallowest possible layer—while threshold-based metrics remain in the L9–L16 range, confirming that the bias is not an artifact of the originally restricted layer range (L12–L24).\"\n\n## Framing\n- 这是对现有 Contribution 1 的加强证据，不是新 claim\n- 强调\"补全浅层后 metric-bias pattern 更加极端\"\n- 不夸大——crossing_rate 选 L0 是因为浅层表征压缩导致 crossing events 多，这个机制在论文中已有解释\n\n## 页面限制\n主文本加 1-2 句（~30 words），详细 table 放 Appendix。应该不影响 8 页限制。\n\n完成后 git commit，汇报修改位置。",
  "project": "contrastive_trace_safety"
}
```

原 L12-24 range 中 BA-best 层为 L14（exp_r14_a1_ba_layer），全 sweep 找到 L12（W=1-5 均为 L12）。差异可能源于：(1) 不同的数据 split 策略，(2) val/test 划分差异。方向一致（mid-depth），不构成矛盾。\n  - 全 sweep 确认 L0-L11 不包含 BA-optimal 层（BA 最优仍在 L12），验证了原 L12-24 restricted range 并未遗漏 BA 最优点。\n\n- [severity: info] **对论文叙事的增值**：R1-8B（25 层）vs 32B 模型（64 层）的 shallow bias 强度差异可解释为：(1) 层数少导致 \"shallow\" 和 \"deep\" 的分界更模糊，(2) 8B 模型的 crossing_rate L0 选择虽然存在但对 composite 的拉力不足以翻转整体排序。这为论文增加了 \"metric bias 与模型深度正相关\" 的 nuance。\n\n### 待修正\n1. Registry spec.checkpoint_path 和 log_path 应更新为 `artifacts/r1_8b_full_layer_sweep/`\n2. 论文引用 W=15 shallow bias 时应注明效应量极小（Δ=0.003）\n\n### 额外确认\nsplit_half_ot7b registry 数值已修正为正确值（half1_precision_layer=20, half2_precision_layer=26, half1_delta_prec=0.262, half2_delta_prec=0.154）。此前多次报告的问题已解决。"

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — r1_8b_full_layer_sweep

**结论**: WARNING（有条件通过）

### 核实方法
- 读取远程 report.txt 和 layer_sweep_results.json
- 对照 registry 条目（status=DONE, 已从 QUEUED 更新）
- 交叉验证与既有实验数据一致性

### 发现

- [severity: info] **数据规模与种子一致**：209 traces, seed=42, 60/20/20 split (train=125, val=41, test=43)。与既有 R1-8B 实验完全一致。

- [severity: info] **registry 指标与 report.txt 完全匹配**：best_layers 各 W 值对应关系、shallow_bias_W15_only=true、max_layer_gap_same_model=16 均正确。

- [severity: warning] **Registry spec 路径错误**：spec.checkpoint_path 和 log_path 指向 `artifacts/hidden_states_all25/`，但该目录为空。实际结果在 `artifacts/r1_8b_full_layer_sweep/`。数据可合并自 `hidden_states/`（L12-24）+ `hidden_states_early_r1_8b/`（L0-11），但 registry 应记录实际输出路径以便复现。

- [severity: info] **核心发现对论文 claim 的影响**：
  1. **composite metric 在 W=1,3,5 无 shallow bias**：shallow mean < deep mean（差距 0.009–0.016），与 32B 模型的强 shallow bias 形成对比。只有 W=15 时出现 shallow bias 且仅 0.003 的微弱优势。
  2. **crossing_rate 在所有 W 值一律选 L0**：这是论文 "crossing_rate 驱动 shallow bias" 论点的最强单一数据点。
  3. **同一模型内 metric 间最大层差=16（L0 vs L16）**：虽然不如 32B 的 L0 vs L63 那么极端，但仍然显著。

- [severity: warning] **W=15 shallow bias 的统计强度存疑**：shallow mean=0.7480 vs deep mean=0.7448，差距仅 0.003。在 43 个 test traces 上，这个差异可能处于噪声水平。论文如果要引用 "W=15 出现 shallow bias"，需要明确标注边际效应大小，避免 overclaim。

- [severity: info] **Text probe 恒选 L0 的解释**：报告中 text probe 在所有层显示相同值（如 W=1 全部 0.5917）——这是预期行为（MiniLM embedding 与 HS layer 选择无关），L0 只是 tie-breaking 的第一个层。报告格式已正确反映此特性。

- [severity: info] **与既有实验的一致性验证**：
  - 原 L12-24 range 中 BA-best 层为 L14（exp_r14_a1_ba_layer），全 sweep 找到 L12（W=1-5 均为 L12）。差异可能源于：(1) 不同的数据 split 策略，(2) val/test 划分差异。方向一致（mid-depth），不构成矛盾。
  - 全 sweep 确认 L0-L11 不包含 BA-optimal 层（BA 最优仍在 L12），验证了原 L12-24 restricted range 并未遗漏 BA 最优点。

- [severity: info] **对论文叙事的增值**：R1-8B（25 层）vs 32B 模型（64 层）的 shallow bias 强度差异可解释为：(1) 层数少导致 "shallow" 和 "deep" 的分界更模糊，(2) 8B 模型的 crossing_rate L0 选择虽然存在但对 composite 的拉力不足以翻转整体排序。这为论文增加了 "metric bias 与模型深度正相关" 的 nuance。

### 待修正
1. Registry spec.checkpoint_path 和 log_path 应更新为 `artifacts/r1_8b_full_layer_sweep/`
2. 论文引用 W=15 shallow bias 时应注明效应量极小（Δ=0.003）

### 额外确认
split_half_ot7b registry 数值已修正为正确值（half1_precision_layer=20, half2_precision_layer=26, half1_delta_prec=0.262, half2_delta_prec=0.154）。此前多次报告的问题已解决。

The Reviewer's distinction between R1-8B and 32B models on shallow bias is important to capture—it's not a universal pattern.

I should send a clarification to Paper Agent about these nuances before the write-up gets finalized.

{
  "text": "[Patch 补充] R1-8B 全层 sweep — Reviewer 修正\n\nReviewer 审查后有一个重要 caveat，请在写入时注意：\n\n**W=15 composite shallow bias 效应极小（Δ=0.003），不要作为主要证据。** shallow mean=0.748 vs deep mean=0.745，在 43 个 test traces 上可能是噪声。\n\n**正确的 framing（替代我上条指令中的部分内容）：**\n\n1. **主要证据是 crossing_rate → L0**：所有 4 个 window 下 crossing_rate 都选 L0，这是 metric-bias 的最强单一数据点。Focus on this。\n\n2. **composite 在 R1-8B 上并未出现强 shallow bias**：W=1,3,5 时 composite best layer 仍在 L12-L16（deep），只有 W=15 略微下移到 L9 且效应量极小。这与 32B 模型的强 shallow bias 形成对比——说明 metric bias 与模型深度可能正相关。\n\n3. **16 层差距（L0 vs L16）**是 crossing_rate vs precision 的差距，不是 composite vs other metrics 的差距。措辞要精确。\n\n**建议的主文措辞调整：**\n\"Full-layer analysis (L0–L24) reveals that crossing-rate consistently selects L0 regardless of window size, while threshold-based metrics select L9–L16—a 16-layer divergence driven by crossing-rate's sensitivity to shallow-layer representation compression.\"\n\n不要说 \"composite shows shallow bias for R1-8B\"——这在大部分 window 下不成立。\n\nAppendix table 中如果列 shallow/deep mean，W=15 的 Δ=0.003 要标注 \"marginal, within noise\"。",
  "project": "contrastive_trace_safety"
}

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Metric-induced layer selection bias**: crossing-rate composite systematically selects shallow layers; confirmed now with full L0-L24 data showing crossing_rate → L0 across all windows

   - **Multi-metric layer ensemble**: 3-layer ensemble averaging predictions from precision-best, BA-best, FPR-best layers — 4/4 models significant vs text (p ≤ 0.002)
   - **IAA (Inter-Annotator Agreement)**: HarmThoughts has Fleiss' κ=0.74-0.80 but only on 13/1081 traces; CP is a derived construct from behavioral labels
   - **CP definition mismatch**: Critical issue where "first commit" ≠ "safety permanently ceases" — fixed in IAA prompt
   - **ReasoningShield debunked**: Claimed sentence-level annotations are actually trace-level; r1-8b model identity uncertain (Qwen vs Llama)
   - **AdvBench pipeline**: Complete end-to-end pipeline for second dataset validation: generate_traces → annotate_cp → convert_annotations → extract_hs → data_loader → probe analysis
   - **Evidence gradient**: Three tiers — cross-validated (7-8B, nested CV), significant/supported (HB pass, no nested CV), exploratory (†, small sample)

        "content": "---\nname: review-report-action\ndescription: '当 Research Director 收到 Review 阶段回传的审稿报告时,立即使用本 skill。先做 Holistic Pass(识别 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE),再做 Per-Comment Action Matrix。禁止\"已知悉\"整体回复。'\nalways: false\nroles:\n  - director\n---\n\n# Review Report Action Matrix\n\n## 硬约束\n收到审稿报告后**禁止**整体性 \"已知悉\" 回复。必须**先做 Holistic Pass,再做 Per-Comment Action Matrix**。\n\n## Step 1: Holistic Pass（整体审视，action matrix 之前必做）\n\n审稿意见不是独立的,经常多条指向同一个系统性问题。切碎成多条独立 action 会漏掉根因。\n\n1. **聚类**：把所有审稿意见按主题聚类（method design / experiment design / claim strength / writing clarity / novelty / related work）\n2. **识别系统性问题**：任何一个 cluster 含 ≥ 3 条意见 → 标记为 `[SYSTEMIC_ISSUE]`,不再切碎成独立 action,作为单独根因处理\n3. **矩阵回溯**：如果 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE 指向 core method design → 立即触发 `experiment-matrix` skill 回顾:\n   - 现有实验矩阵在新的 method 理解下还有几格有效？\n   - 哪些之前的 positive 实验可能因此失效（method 基础变了）？\n   - 哪些格子要重新跑？\n4. **全局影响评估**：SYSTEMIC_ISSUE 是否影响论文 core claim？如果是 → 必须子调 `red-section` 做 framing 评估,然后再动 action matrix\n\n**Holistic Pass 输出写入 ROADMAP.md 的\"审稿回溯·系统性问题\"section**,先于 action matrix。\n\n## Step 2: Action Matrix 格式\n\n| 审稿意见 ID | 内容摘要 | 类别 | 动作 | 负责人 | ETA |\n|-----------|--------|------|------|-------|-----|\n| R1.1 | 缺某 ablation | 补实验 | 让 Agent 铺 exp_XXX | Agent | 2d |\n| R1.2 | Claim 过强 | 改 paper | 调 red-section 重写 intro | Agent | 1d |\n| R1.3 | 某 method 解释不清 | 写 rebuttal | Director 写 rebuttal 论点 | Director | 1d |\n| R2.1 | Core claim 不成立 | framing 调整 | 子调 red-section + 改矩阵 | Agent + Director | 5d |\n\n## 类别\n- **补实验**：必须让 Agent 铺实验（可与 rebuttal 并行）\n- **改 paper**：让 Agent 调 red-section 后改章节\n- **写 rebuttal**：Director 主笔\n- **拒绝**：有理由的拒绝（写入 action matrix 的\"拒绝理由\"字段）\n\n## 产出\n- action matrix 写入 ROADMAP.md 的\"审稿回溯\"section\n- 决策日志同步（`log_decision`）\n- 补实验条目立即调度（不等 rebuttal 写完）\n- 如果有 \"framing 调整\" 类别 → 子调 red-section skill\n\n## Step 3: 同步 Issue Tracker（必做）\n\naction matrix 定稿后,同步更新跨轮次 Issue Tracker `artifacts/review_issues.yaml`:\n\n1. 用 `read_local_file(\"projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml\")` 读当前 tracker（不存在则视为空）\n2. 对 action matrix 中每条:\n   - 找到对应的 issue ID（同一 cluster / 相似 summary）\n   - 把 `status` 从 `open` 翻到 `in_progress`\n   - 在 `action_items` 里追加本轮 action（含 round / action / assigned_to / status）\n3. 对上轮标 `in_progress` 且本轮 Reviewer 已确认修复（`[RESOLVED]`）的:\n   - `status` 改为 `resolved`,记 `resolution_round` 和 `resolution`\n4. 调 `update_review_issues(project, issues_yaml=完整 YAML)` 写回\n\n## 提交下一轮审稿（如适用）\n\n调 `submit_paper_to_review` 时根据情景设 `recommended_review_mode`:\n\n- **`continue`**：刚迭代修了上轮问题,需要 Reviewer 带着上轮记忆验证修复效果\n- **`fresh`**：做了重大 reframing / 距上次审稿 >48h / 论文结构大改 → 想要不受干扰的客观评估\n\n同时在 `mode_reason` 写一句推荐理由。人类仍可在审批卡片上覆盖。\n",
        "numLines": 70,

1.5d）：N3 收窄 32B claims + W1-W7 修复 + 4 引用   -  第二数据集线 （Agent, 2.3d）：用 R1-8B 在 XSTest/AdvBench 上生成 traces → LLM 标注 CP → 5-metric layer sweep → 只验证 Contribution 1（composite 是否仍选 shallow layer） -  如果 5/23 shallow bias 是 metric 固有属性，复现概率高。两条线并行不浪费时间，失败了也能退回 B。\\\", \\\"rootId\\\": \\\"\\\"}, {\\\"messageId\\\": \\\"om_x100b6ffdeaa8e4a0c329ed507748e08\\\", \\\"senderId\\\": \\\"cli_a974558fe4b81bc9\\\", \\\"createTime\\\": \\\"1779210822410\\\", \\\"msgType\\\": \\\"text\\\", \\\"content\\\": \\\"等待 Reviewer 对 R21 战略方案的独立评估，再做最终决策。\\\", \\\"rootId\\\": \\\"\\\"}, {\\\"messageId\\\": \\\"om_x100b6ffdd46efca0c37e12f45859b6a\\\", \\\"senderId\\\": \\\"cli_a974558fe4b81bc9\\\", \\\"createTime\\\": \\\"1779210666345\\\", \\\"msgType\\\": \\\"post\\\", \\\"content\\\": \\\"🔄 [ 重新审稿] 📋  [审稿群 → Research Director] 审稿报告 Round 21 \\\\n Overall:  3.0 (Findings) Consolidated Review Report — Round 21 (Fresh Review) Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models Venue : EMNLP 2026 (ARR May 2026 cycle) |  Deadline : 2026-05-25 |  Reviewers : 2 |  Rounds : 1 (converged) Summary This paper investigates how the choice of layer selection metric systematically biases which transformer layer is chosen for hidden-state (HS) safety probes in reasoning models, and how this bias can reverse HS vs. text probe conclusions. Across four reasoning LLMs (3 Qwen-based, 1 Llama) on the HarmThoughts benchmark, the authors show that crossing-rate composite metrics select shallow layers (inflating threshold crossings due to representation compression), while threshold-based metrics select deep layers where HS advantages of +19.2 to +21.9pp emerge against a 384d MiniLM text encoder. The HS advantage is encoder-conditional (1/4 models survives HB correction at 1024d) and partially position-mediated (2/4 models). A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic selection. The paper includes extensive negative results and an unusually honest limitations section. Strengths S1. Important methodological finding.  The identification of metric-induced layer selection bias as a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse HS vs. text conclusions (Table 2) has lasting value for the probing literature. The five-metric ablation with random-label control (Appendix B.1) demonstrates this is a property of representation geometry, not label-specific. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S2. Statistical rigor far exceeding peer work.  Holm-Bonferroni correction, nested 5-fold CV, BCa bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples with trace-level clustering), permutation tests, MDE calculation (Appendix AA), and explicit evidence gradient classification. Trace-level bootstrap correctly handles temporal dependence between steps. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S3. Systematic negative results.  Section 4.6 and Table 20 report failures of 7 alternative methods (BOCPD, CUSUM, Mahalanobis, RepE, MLP, CCS, cross-model transfer) and distill four signal characteristic requirements. This defines method boundaries for future work. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S4. Scientific integrity.  M002 bug disclosure (Appendix N), explicit exploratory (†) markers for underpowered 32B results, seed-sensitivity reporting for R1-8B FPR, and one of the most comprehensive limitations sections in recent NLP papers. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S5. Format compliance.  8-page main text, correct ACL template with  [review]  option, proper anonymization (\\\\\\\"Anonymous ARR Submission\\\\\\\"). [ R1, R2 consensus ] Weaknesses Must-Fix (3 issues — reviewer consensus) N1 [critical] Single dataset severely limits external validity of the core methodological claim. -  All experiments use only HarmThoughts. The paper positions Contribution 1 as \\\\\\\"follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts\\\\\\\" (Section 5) but provides no second-dataset validation. The cosine distance geometric argument (Appendix C) is computed on HarmThoughts traces only. HarmThoughts' right-skewed CP distribution and specific category composition may drive the observed patterns. Claim scope substantially exceeds evidence scope. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. This is the highest-priority issue. -  Action: Validate Table 2 core pattern on at least one additional safety benchmark, or significantly narrow generalizability claims. N2 [critical] Missing inter-annotator agreement (IAA) undermines CP annotation quality foundation. -  Section 7 states: \\\\\\\"Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations.\\\\\\\" The entire experimental framework depends on CP annotation quality. QwQ-32B's HS lead time of -1.2 to +1.9 steps falls within plausible annotation error. Appendix J's perturbation analysis tests random noise but cannot capture systematic annotation bias (consistently early/late CP placement). -  R1: Must-Fix. R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J provides indirect evidence). Director resolution:  Must-Fix  — CP is the paper's foundational concept; marginal 32B results are vulnerable to annotation uncertainty within noise range. -  Action: Report IAA if obtainable; conduct small-scale re-annotation study; or discuss expected noise magnitude based on annotation methodology. N3 [critical] Tiny 32B sample size (n=19-20) bears excessive inferential load. -  MDE at 80% power is 8.6-9.5pp (Table 35), far exceeding R1-32B's residualized +1.9pp. With W=5, probe input is 25,600-dimensional with only ~ 60 training samples — overfitting risk is severe even with L2 regularization. Despite correct † markers, the paper draws multiple substantive conclusions from these micro-samples: QwQ-32B \\\\\\\"position-competitive,\\\\\\\" R1-32B \\\\\\\"position-mediated,\\\\\\\" 32B cross-model divergence analysis, quadratic position absorption. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. -  Action: Collect more 32B data; restrict 32B discussion to purely descriptive observations; or reposition as pilot study with explicit power requirements. Should-Fix (7 issues) W1 [warning] Position baseline threatens core HS advantage — 2/4 models absorbed or severely weakened. \\\\n QwQ-32B's HS advantage fully absorbed by quadratic position term (Table 26); R1-32B residualized gap +1.9pp (p=0.914). The paper labels quadratic control as \\\\\\\"sensitivity analysis, not primary specification\\\\\\\" (Section 5) without sufficient justification for why linear should be preferred. HS probes may simply be better position encoders than text probes rather than capturing genuine safety-relevant features. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W2 [warning] MiniLM-384d is a weak baseline — capacity mismatch makes HS advantage partly expected. \\\\n HS representations from 7-32B models (3584-5120d) compared against 384d/22M-parameter MiniLM. At 1024d BGE-large, only 1/4 models passes HB. E5-Mistral-7B (2/4 HB) shows monotonic gradient: stronger encoder → smaller HS advantage. PCA capacity matching preserves advantage but does not fully resolve quality differences. Abstract leads with \\\\\\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\\\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against the weakest baseline. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W3 [warning] HB correction scope may be insufficient. \\\\n HB covers 12 final tests (3 metrics × 4 models), but total researcher degrees of freedom include 5 metrics × 7 W values × 28-64 layers × multiple thresholds × 2 split ratios. Evidence gradient classification itself is a post-hoc decision. Nested CV addresses within-loop selection for 7-8B but not the full exploration space. [ R1: Should-Fix, R2: Minor — Director keeps at Should-Fix given cumulative exploration acknowledged in Section 7 ] W4 [warning] 3/4 models share Qwen architecture — limited architectural diversity. \\\\n Only R1-8B (Llama) provides cross-architecture evidence, and it happens to show the strongest HS advantage. R1-32B and QwQ-32B share Qwen-32B base but show qualitatively different probe characteristics — training method may matter more than architecture, but tested on only one architecture pair. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W5 [warning] CP definition creates conceptual tension with online detection motivation. \\\\n CP requires observing the full trace (\\\\\\\"the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases\\\\\\\"), yet the introduction frames the work toward \\\\\\\"production detectors.\\\\\\\" R1 flagged this as Should-Fix; R2 rebutted (standard probing paradigm). Director resolution: the probing setup is methodologically valid, but the introduction should more clearly distinguish retrospective classification from online detection. [ R1 raised, R2 partial rebut ] W6 [warning] FPR 11-50% inconsistent with deployment narrative. \\\\n 76.7% oracle gap (Appendix I) further highlights the performance ceiling. The paper acknowledges in Limitations that methods \\\\\\\"characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors,\\\\\\\" but this caveat should appear earlier. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W7 [warning] \\\\\\\"Confirmatory\\\\\\\" terminology potentially misleading. \\\\n R2 flagged as Must-Fix (no pre-registration); R1 rebutted as Should-Fix (paper defines it operationally in footnote 1 as \\\\\\\"nested CV validated,\\\\\\\" not in the pre-registration sense). Director resolution:  Should-Fix  — the paper does provide a clear operational definition, making this a terminology issue rather than a methodological flaw. Renaming to \\\\\\\"validated\\\\\\\" or \\\\\\\"cross-validated\\\\\\\" would resolve the concern without affecting any substantive conclusion. Reviewer Consensus Both reviewers independently agree on: -  [ AGREE]  N1 : Single dataset is the most critical limitation (both marked critical without seeing each other's initial review) -  [ AGREE]  N3 : 32B sample size inadequate for the inferential load placed on it -  [ AGREE]  W1 : Position confound is serious for 2/4 models -  [ AGREE]  W2 : Encoder capacity mismatch partially explains HS advantage -  [ AGREE]  W4 : Architecture diversity insufficient (3/4 Qwen) -  [ AGREE]  S1-S4 : All strengths confirmed by both reviewers -  [ AGREE ] Methodological contribution (Contribution 1) has lasting community value -  [ AGREE ] Paper should be reframed with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization Reviewer Divergence D1: Ensemble evaluation. -  R1: Ensemble claim is well-supported — the claim is metric-agnostic selection (vs. text: 4/4 significant, p≤0.002), not superiority over best single metric. [ REBUT ] -  R2: Ens-Best is not significant (p>0.27), incremental value indistinguishable from zero. [ REBUT to R1's S5 ] -  Director note: Both interpretations are valid. Ensemble provides practical metric-agnostic value but does not improve over oracle single-metric selection. The claim boundary should be stated more clearly. D2: IAA severity. -  R1: Must-Fix (CP annotation quality is foundational; marginal 32B results vulnerable) -  R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J perturbation provides indirect evidence; CP comes from original benchmark) -  Director resolution: Must-Fix (see N2 above). D3: CP definition circularity. -  R1: Should-Fix (conceptual tension with online detection) -  R2: Rebut (standard probing paradigm; paper is analysis not deployment) -  Director resolution: Should-Fix (W5 above), but acknowledges R2's point that the probing paradigm itself is standard. Questions for Authors 1.  If Table 2's metric-layer interaction pattern were tested on WildJailbreak or another safety dataset, would it persist? This is the single most important open question. 2.  Was the \\\\\\\"confirmatory / significant / exploratory\\\\\\\" evidence tier classification determined before or after seeing results? If post-hoc, how does this not constitute additional reporting degrees of freedom? 3.  For QwQ-32B, why should the linear position specification be preferred over quadratic? Can you rule out that HS probes are simply better position encoders rather than capturing genuine safety content? 4.  With W=5 and 5120d hidden states, probe input is 25,600-dimensional but training set is ~ 60 samples. How do you ensure L2 regularization is sufficient to prevent overfitting? 5.  Are results stable across different harmful categories within HarmThoughts? Could the HS advantage be driven by specific attack types? 6.  R1 correctly noted that R2 misattributed R1-32B's 3.0pp selectivity to R1-8B. Please confirm: R1-8B selectivity is 14.1pp/22.9pp (well above threshold), and R1-32B residualized selectivity is 3.0pp (below threshold)? Missing References 1.  Zou et al. (2024) , \\\\\\\"Improving Alignment and Robustness with Circuit Breakers\\\\\\\" — representation-level safety intervention, directly relevant to causal validation discussion 2.  Templeton et al. (2024) , \\\\\\\"Scaling Monosemanticity\\\\\\\" — large-scale feature extraction for understanding safety representations 3.  Gelman & Loken (2013) , \\\\\\\"The garden of forking paths\\\\\\\" — already in bib ( gelman2013garden ) but never cited; Section 5's \\\\\\\"researcher degree of freedom\\\\\\\" discussion should cite this foundational work 4. Visual Layout Check (非评分项) Status: Timeout  (815 seconds elapsed without completion). Visual review agent did not return results in time. Known visual issues from reviewer comments: -  Figure 1 (p.6) heat map too small — axis labels and metric names hard to read (R1 m2) -  Figure 1 not colorblind-friendly (R2 minor) -  Abstract information density excessive for first-time readers (R1 m1, R2 minor) Overall Assessment Score: 3.0 (Findings) \\\\n Confidence: 4/5 Scoring Rationale Contribution 1 (metric-induced layer selection bias)  is the paper's core strength: a methodologically sound finding with practical community value that both reviewers recognized as important. However, it is validated on only one dataset, preventing confidence in the generalization claim. Contribution 2 (HS vs. text empirical comparison)  is substantially weakened by multiple compounding limitations: -  Single dataset (no external validity) -  32B results statistically underpowered (n=19-20, effects below MDE) -  Position confound absorbs HS advantage for 2/4 models -  Encoder-conditional advantage (1/4 HB at 1024d BGE-large) -  Missing IAA for CP annotations The paper's exceptional statistical rigor, honest limitations disclosure, and systematic negative results elevate it above typical Findings-level work in methodology. However, when half the results require † markers, footnotes, and multi-layer qualifications, the empirical signal is too fragile for Conference acceptance. Findings recommendation : Reframe with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization on one benchmark. The methodological warning about layer selection bias has sufficient community value for Findings. Conference acceptance would require : (a) second dataset validating layer selection bias generalizability, (b) larger 32B samples or withdrawal of 32B inferential claims, (c) stronger position control justification. Individual Reviewer Scores The 0.5 gap stems from: (1) R1 gives more weight to Contribution 1's independent methodological value; (2) R2 penalizes \\\\\\\"confirmatory\\\\\\\" terminology more severely; (3) R2 rates excitement lower (2.5/5 vs R1's 3.0/5). On substantive weaknesses (N1, N3, W1, W2, W4), both reviewers are in close agreement. Director's consolidated score follows R2's assessment:  3.0 (Findings) . 战略方案的独立评估，再做最终决策。\\\\n[05-19 17:11] cli_a974558fe4b81bc9: 🔄 [ 重新审稿] 📋  [审稿群 → Research Director] 审稿报告 Round 21 \\\\n Overall:  3.0 (Findings) Consolidated Review Report — Round 21 (Fresh Review) Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models Venue : EMNLP 2026 (ARR May 2026 cycle) |  Deadline : 2026-05-25 |  Reviewers : 2 |  Rounds : 1 (converged) Summary This paper investigates how the choice of layer selection metric systematically biases which transformer layer is chosen for hidden-state (HS) safety probes in reasoning models, and how this bias can reverse HS vs. text probe conclusions. Across four reasoning LLMs (3 Qwen-based, 1 Llama) on the HarmThoughts benchmark, the authors show that crossing-rate composite metrics select shallow layers (inflating threshold crossings due to representation compression), while threshold-based metrics select deep layers where HS advantages of +19.2 to +21.9pp emerge against a 384d MiniLM text encoder. The HS advantage is encoder-conditional (1/4 models survives HB correction at 1024d) and partially position-mediated (2/4 models). A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic selection. The paper includes extensive negative results and an unusually honest limitations section. Strengths S1. Important methodological finding.  The identification of metric-induced layer selection bias as a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse HS vs. text conclusions (Table 2) has lasting value for the probing literature. The five-metric ablation with random-label control (Appendix B.1) demonstrates this is a property of representation geometry, not label-specific. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S2. Statistical rigor far exceeding peer work.  Holm-Bonferroni correction, nested 5-fold CV, BCa bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples with trace-level clustering), permutation tests, MDE calculation (Appendix AA), and explicit evidence gradient classification. Trace-level bootstrap correctly handles temporal dependence between steps. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S3. Systematic negative results.  Section 4.6 and Table 20 report failures of 7 alternative methods (BOCPD, CUSUM, Mahalanobis, RepE, MLP, CCS, cross-model transfer) and distill four signal characteristic requirements. This defines method boundaries for future work. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S4. Scientific integrity.  M002 bug disclosure (Appendix N), explicit exploratory (†) markers for underpowered 32B results, seed-sensitivity reporting for R1-8B FPR, and one of the most comprehensive limitations sections in recent NLP papers. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S5. Format compliance.  8-page main text, correct ACL template with  [review]  option, proper anonymization (\\\\\\\"Anonymous ARR Submission\\\\\\\"). [ R1, R2 consensus ] Weaknesses Must-Fix (3 issues — reviewer consensus) N1 [critical] Single dataset severely limits external validity of the core methodological claim. -  All experiments use only HarmThoughts. The paper positions Contribution 1 as \\\\\\\"follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts\\\\\\\" (Section 5) but provides no second-dataset validation. The cosine distance geometric argument (Appendix C) is computed on HarmThoughts traces only. HarmThoughts' right-skewed CP distribution and specific category composition may drive the observed patterns. Claim scope substantially exceeds evidence scope. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. This is the highest-priority issue. -  Action: Validate Table 2 core pattern on at least one additional safety benchmark, or significantly narrow generalizability claims. N2 [critical] Missing inter-annotator agreement (IAA) undermines CP annotation quality foundation. -  Section 7 states: \\\\\\\"Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations.\\\\\\\" The entire experimental framework depends on CP annotation quality. QwQ-32B's HS lead time of -1.2 to +1.9 steps falls within plausible annotation error. Appendix J's perturbation analysis tests random noise but cannot capture systematic annotation bias (consistently early/late CP placement). -  R1: Must-Fix. R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J provides indirect evidence). Director resolution:  Must-Fix  — CP is the paper's foundational concept; marginal 32B results are vulnerable to annotation uncertainty within noise range. -  Action: Report IAA if obtainable; conduct small-scale re-annotation study; or discuss expected noise magnitude based on annotation methodology. N3 [critical] Tiny 32B sample size (n=19-20) bears excessive inferential load. -  MDE at 80% power is 8.6-9.5pp (Table 35), far exceeding R1-32B's residualized +1.9pp. With W=5, probe input is 25,600-dimensional with only ~ 60 training samples — overfitting risk is severe even with L2 regularization. Despite correct † markers, the paper draws multiple substantive conclusions from these micro-samples: QwQ-32B \\\\\\\"position-competitive,\\\\\\\" R1-32B \\\\\\\"position-mediated,\\\\\\\" 32B cross-model divergence analysis, quadratic position absorption. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. -  Action: Collect more 32B data; restrict 32B discussion to purely descriptive observations; or reposition as pilot study with explicit power requirements. Should-Fix (7 issues) W1 [warning] Position baseline threatens core HS advantage — 2/4 models absorbed or severely weakened. \\\\n QwQ-32B's HS advantage fully absorbed by quadratic position term (Table 26); R1-32B residualized gap +1.9pp (p=0.914). The paper labels quadratic control as \\\\\\\"sensitivity analysis, not primary specification\\\\\\\" (Section 5) without sufficient justification for why linear should be preferred. HS probes may simply be better position encoders than text probes rather than capturing genuine safety-relevant features. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W2 [warning] MiniLM-384d is a weak baseline — capacity mismatch makes HS advantage partly expected. \\\\n HS representations from 7-32B models (3584-5120d) compared against 384d/22M-parameter MiniLM. At 1024d BGE-large, only 1/4 models passes HB. E5-Mistral-7B (2/4 HB) shows monotonic gradient: stronger encoder → smaller HS advantage. PCA capacity matching preserves advantage but does not fully resolve quality differences. Abstract leads with \\\\\\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\\\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against the weakest baseline. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W3 [warning] HB correction scope may be insufficient. \\\\n HB covers 12 final tests (3 metrics × 4 models), but total researcher degrees of freedom include 5 metrics × 7 W values × 28-64 layers × multiple thresholds × 2 split ratios. Evidence gradient classification itself is a post-hoc decision. Nested CV addresses within-loop selection for 7-8B but not the full exploration space. [ R1: Should-Fix, R2: Minor — Director keeps at Should-Fix given cumulative exploration acknowledged in Section 7 ] W4 [warning] 3/4 models share Qwen architecture — limited architectural diversity. \\\\n Only R1-8B (Llama) provides cross-architecture evidence, and it happens to show the strongest HS advantage. R1-32B and QwQ-32B share Qwen-32B base but show qualitatively different probe characteristics — training method may matter more than architecture, but tested on only one architecture pair. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W5 [warning] CP definition creates conceptual tension with online detection motivation. \\\\n CP requires observing the full trace (\\\\\\\"the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases\\\\\\\"), yet the introduction frames the work toward \\\\\\\"production detectors.\\\\\\\" R1 flagged this as Should-Fix; R2 rebutted (standard probing paradigm). Director resolution: the probing setup is methodologically valid, but the introduction should more clearly distinguish retrospective classification from online detection. [ R1 raised, R2 partial rebut ] W6 [warning] FPR 11-50% inconsistent with deployment narrative. \\\\n 76.7% oracle gap (Appendix I) further highlights the performance ceiling. The paper acknowledges in Limitations that methods \\\\\\\"characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors,\\\\\\\" but this caveat should appear earlier. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W7 [warning] \\\\\\\"Confirmatory\\\\\\\" terminology potentially misleading. \\\\n R2 flagged as Must-Fix (no pre-registration); R1 rebutted as Should-Fix (paper defines it operationally in footnote 1 as \\\\\\\"nested CV validated,\\\\\\\" not in the pre-registration sense). Director resolution:  Should-Fix  — the paper does provide a clear operational definition, making this a terminology issue rather than a methodological flaw. Renaming to \\\\\\\"validated\\\\\\\" or \\\\\\\"cross-validated\\\\\\\" would resolve the concern without affecting any substantive conclusion. Reviewer Consensus Both reviewers independently agree on: -  [ AGREE]  N1 : Single dataset is the most critical limitation (both marked critical without seeing each other's initial review) -  [ AGREE]  N3 : 32B sample size inadequate for the inferential load placed on it -  [ AGREE]  W1 : Position confound is serious for 2/4 models -  [ AGREE]  W2 : Encoder capacity mismatch partially explains HS advantage -  [ AGREE]  W4 : Architecture diversity insufficient (3/4 Qwen) -  [ AGREE]  S1-S4 : All strengths confirmed by both reviewers -  [ AGREE ] Methodological contribution (Contribution 1) has lasting community value -  [ AGREE ] Paper should be reframed with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization Reviewer Divergence D1: Ensemble evaluation. -  R1: Ensemble claim is well-supported — the claim is metric-agnostic selection (vs. text: 4/4 significant, p≤0.002), not superiority over best single metric. [ REBUT ] -  R2: Ens-Best is not significant (p>0.27), incremental value indistinguishable from zero. [ REBUT to R1's S5 ] -  Director note: Both interpretations are valid. Ensemble provides practical metric-agnostic value but does not improve over oracle single-metric selection. The claim boundary should be stated more clearly. D2: IAA severity. -  R1: Must-Fix (CP annotation quality is foundational; marginal 32B results vulnerable) -  R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J perturbation provides indirect evidence; CP comes from original benchmark) -  Director resolution: Must-Fix (see N2 above). D3: CP definition circularity. -  R1: Should-Fix (conceptual tension with online detection) -  R2: Rebut (standard probing paradigm; paper is analysis not deployment) -  Director resolution: Should-Fix (W5 above), but acknowledges R2's point that the probing paradigm itself is standard. Questions for Authors 1.  If Table 2's metric-layer interaction pattern were tested on WildJailbreak or another safety dataset, would it persist? This is the single most important open question. 2.  Was the \\\\\\\"confirmatory / significant / exploratory\\\\\\\" evidence tier classification determined before or after seeing results? If post-hoc, how does this not constitute additional reporting degrees of freedom? 3.  For QwQ-32B, why should the linear position specification be preferred over quadratic? Can you rule out that HS probes are simply better position encoders rather than capturing genuine safety content? 4.  With W=5 and 5120d hidden states, probe input is 25,600-dimensional but training set is ~ 60 samples. How do you ensure L2 regularization is sufficient to prevent overfitting? 5.  Are results stable across different harmful categories within HarmThoughts? Could the HS advantage be driven by specific attack types? 6.  R1 correctly noted that R2 misattributed R1-32B's 3.0pp selectivity to R1-8B. Please confirm: R1-8B selectivity is 14.1pp/22.9pp (well above threshold), and R1-32B residualized selectivity is 3.0pp (below threshold)? Missing References 1.  Zou et al. (2024) , \\\\\\\"Improving Alignment and Robustness with Circuit Breakers\\\\\\\" — representation-level safety intervention, directly relevant to causal validation discussion 2.  Templeton et al. (2024) , \\\\\\\"Scaling Monosemanticity\\\\\\\" — large-scale feature extraction for understanding safety representations 3.  Gelman & Loken (2013) , \\\\\\\"The garden of forking paths\\\\\\\" — already in bib ( gelman2013garden ) but never cited; Section 5's \\\\\\\"researcher degree of freedom\\\\\\\" discussion should cite this foundational work 4.  Turner et al. (2023) , activation addition — relevant to activation steering for safety External Review (Stanford Agentic Reviewer) Status: Skipped.  External review request was sent but skipped by user (2026-05-19T17:01:27Z). No external review data available for this round. Visual Layout Check (非评分项) Status: Timeout  (815 seconds elapsed without completion). Visual review agent did not return results in time. Known visual issues from reviewer comments: -  Figure 1 (p.6) heat map too small — axis labels and metric names hard to read (R1 m2) -  Figure 1 not colorblind-friendly (R2 minor) -  Abstract information density excessive for first-time readers (R1 m1, R2 minor) Overall Assessment Score: 3.0 (Findings) \\\\n Confidence: 4/5 Scoring Rationale Contribution 1 (metric-induced layer selection bias)  is the paper's core strength: a methodologically sound finding with practical community value that both reviewers recognized as important. However, it is validated on only one dataset, preventing confidence in the generalization claim. Contribution 2 (HS vs. text empirical comparison)  is substantially weakened by multiple compounding limitations: -  Single dataset (no external validity) -  32B results statistically underpowered (n=19-20, effects below MDE) -  Position confound absorbs HS advantage for 2/4 models -  Encoder-conditional advantage (1/4 HB at 1024d BGE-large) -  Missing IAA for CP annotations The paper's exceptional statistical rigor, honest limitations disclosure, and systematic negative results elevate it above typical Findings-level work in methodology. However, when half the results require † markers, footnotes, and multi-layer qualifications, the empirical signal is too fragile for Conference acceptance. Findings recommendation : Reframe with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization on one benchmark. The methodological warning about layer selection bias has sufficient community value for Findings. Conference acceptance would require : (a) second dataset validating layer selection bias generalizability, (b) larger 32B samples or withdrawal of 32B inferential claims, (c) stronger position control justification. Individual Reviewer Scores The 0.5 gap stems from: (1) R1 gives more weight to Contribution 1's independent methodological value; (2) R2 penalizes \\\\\\\"confirmatory\\\\\\\" terminology more severely; (3) R2 rates excitement lower (2.5/5 vs R1's 3.0/5). On substantive weaknesses (N1, N3, W1, W2, W4), both reviewers are in close agreement. Director's consolidated score follows R2's assessment:  3.0 (Findings) . Detailed Comments Abstract -  Information density too high — reads as compressed results summary rather than accessible abstract. Should present core finding first, then add qualifications incrementally. [ R1, R2 ] -  Leads with \\\\\\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\\\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against MiniLM-384d. Encoder-quality boundary should be reflected in abstract. [ R1, R2 ] Section 1 (Introduction) -  Problem framing is clear: methodological question (layer selection sensitivity) before empirical question (HS vs. text) -  \\\\\\\"Before building production detectors\\\\\\\" creates deployment expectations inconsistent with 11-50% FPR reality. [ R1 ] -  Contribution 2 listing reads defensively — too many qualifications in the contribution statement itself. [ R1 ] -  \\\\\\\"We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusions\\\\\\\" — R2 flagged as over-strong; R1 defended with Table 2 evidence. Director: the claim is supported but \\\\\\\"a study's\\\\\\\" is ambiguous (could imply published work was reversed). Section 2 (Related Work) -  Comprehensive and well-organized. Correct distinction between attack characterization / detection / mitigation. [ R1 ] -  Chan & Yong (2025) relationship discussed appropriately. [ R1 ] -  \\\\\\\"Alternative Detection Approaches\\\\\\\" paragraph could better distinguish temporal/spatial/cross-model methods. [ R2 ] Section 3 (Methods) -  CP definition (Section 3.1) is concise but oscillation handling needs more discussion. [ R1 ] -  MiniLM baseline weakness should be declared earlier. [ R1 ] -  Five-metric design (Section 3.3) is well-conceived — the methodological core. [ R1 ] -  Bootstrap clustering and HB implementation are correct. [ R1 ] Section 4 (Experiments) -  Table 2 is the most important table and clearly demonstrates the core finding. [ R1, R2 ] -  Section 4.3 position mediation is honest but weakens the central claim — consider giving quadratic control more narrative weight. [ R1 ] -  Section 4.4 32B analysis over-interprets given sample sizes. [ R1 ] -  18/20 model-seed pass rate: the 2 failures should be clearly identified in main text. [ R1 ] Section 5 (Discussion) -  Correct distinction between Contribution 1 (geometric, potentially generalizable) and Contribution 2 (dataset-dependent). [ R1, R2 ] -  \\\\\\\"Researcher degree of freedom\\\\\\\" discussion should cite Gelman & Loken (2013) — already in bib but uncited. [ R1, R2 ] -  Ensemble proof-of-concept adds practical value; claim boundaries should be stated more precisely. [ R1, R2 diverge on evaluation ] Section 6 (Conclusion) -  Concise and appropriately conditional. [ R1 ] Section 7 (Limitations) -  Exceptionally comprehensive: dataset, statistical power, hyperparameters, architecture diversity, CP definition. [ R1, R2 ] -  \\\\\\\"One of the most honest limitations sections\\\\\\\" (R1) — but the volume of limitations is itself a signal about empirical fragility. Appendix (17 pages) -  Extensive robustness checks far exceeding typical NLP papers. [ R1 ] -  Bug disclosure (Appendix N) is excellent scientific practice. [ R1, R2 ] -  Power analysis (Appendix AA) correctly highlights 32B sample inadequacy. [ R1 ] -  Section labels (V, Z, AA) could be more descriptive. 不是因为没承认问题，而是证据本身不够。 新增 3 个 Must-Fix（全部是数据层面，非文本）： -  N1: 单数据集（HarmThoughts）限制外部效度 — 两位 reviewer 独立标 critical -  N2: 缺 inter-annotator agreement (IAA) -  N3: 32B 样本量 n=19-20 承载过多推断 三个选项，请选择方向： (A) 并行冲刺（推荐）— 保留突破 3.5 的可能 -  同时启动两条线：   -  文本修改线 （Paper Agent, 1.5d）：N3 收窄 32B claims + W1-W7 修复 + 4 引用   -  第二数据集线 （Agent, 2.3d）：用 R1-8B 在 XSTest/AdvBench 上生成 traces → LLM 标注 CP → 5-metric layer sweep → 只验证 Contribution 1（composite 是否仍选 shallow layer） -  如果 5/23 前 metric-bias pattern 复现 → 加入论文，可能推到 3.5 -  如果未复现或来不及 → 按纯文本版本提交 -  总耗时 ~ 4.8d，margin 1.2d (B) 接受 Findings，本 cycle 提交 -  只做文本修改（1.5d）+ compile + 提交 ARR -  预期仍 ~ 3.0，Findings 接受取决于 AC -  风险低但突破 3.0 概率 ~ 10-15% (C) 推迟到下一个 ARR cycle -  充分时间做 2-3 个数据集 + IAA + 扩展到非 Qwen 模型 -  风险：Chan & Yong 2025 已存在，更多竞争工作可能出现 我的判断：选 A。  第二数据集实验只验证 metric-bias pattern（不需要完整 HS vs Text 比较），random-CP 实验已证明 composite 的 shallow bias 是 metric 固有属性，复现概率高。两条线并行不浪费时间，失败了也能退回 B。\", \"rootId\": \"\"}, {\"messageId\": \"om_x100b6ffdeaa8e4a0c329ed507748e08\", \"senderId\": \"cli_a974558fe4b81bc9\", \"createTime\": \"1779210822410\", \"msgType\": \"text\", \"content\": \"等待 Reviewer 对 R21 战略方案的独立评估，再做最终决策。\", \"rootId\": \"\"}, {\"messageId\": \"om_x100b6ffdd46efca0c37e12f45859b6a\", \"senderId\": \"cli_a974558fe4b81bc9\", \"createTime\": \"1779210666345\", \"msgType\": \"post\", \"content\": \"🔄 [ 重新审稿] 📋  [审稿群 → Research Director] 审稿报告 Round 21 \\n Overall:  3.0 (Findings) Consolidated Review Report — Round 21 (Fresh Review) Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models Venue : EMNLP 2026 (ARR May 2026 cycle) |  Deadline : 2026-05-25 |  Reviewers : 2 |  Rounds : 1 (converged) Summary This paper investigates how the choice of layer selection metric systematically biases which transformer layer is chosen for hidden-state (HS) safety probes in reasoning models, and how this bias can reverse HS vs. text probe conclusions. Across four reasoning LLMs (3 Qwen-based, 1 Llama) on the HarmThoughts benchmark, the authors show that crossing-rate composite metrics select shallow layers (inflating threshold crossings due to representation compression), while threshold-based metrics select deep layers where HS advantages of +19.2 to +21.9pp emerge against a 384d MiniLM text encoder. The HS advantage is encoder-conditional (1/4 models survives HB correction at 1024d) and partially position-mediated (2/4 models). A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic selection. The paper includes extensive negative results and an unusually honest limitations section. Strengths S1. Important methodological finding.  The identification of metric-induced layer selection bias as a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse HS vs. text conclusions (Table 2) has lasting value for the probing literature. The five-metric ablation with random-label control (Appendix B.1) demonstrates this is a property of representation geometry, not label-specific. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S2. Statistical rigor far exceeding peer work.  Holm-Bonferroni correction, nested 5-fold CV, BCa bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples with trace-level clustering), permutation tests, MDE calculation (Appendix AA), and explicit evidence gradient classification. Trace-level bootstrap correctly handles temporal dependence between steps. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S3. Systematic negative results.  Section 4.6 and Table 20 report failures of 7 alternative methods (BOCPD, CUSUM, Mahalanobis, RepE, MLP, CCS, cross-model transfer) and distill four signal characteristic requirements. This defines method boundaries for future work. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S4. Scientific integrity.  M002 bug disclosure (Appendix N), explicit exploratory (†) markers for underpowered 32B results, seed-sensitivity reporting for R1-8B FPR, and one of the most comprehensive limitations sections in recent NLP papers. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S5. Format compliance.  8-page main text, correct ACL template with  [review]  option, proper anonymization (\\\"Anonymous ARR Submission\\\"). [ R1, R2 consensus ] Weaknesses Must-Fix (3 issues — reviewer consensus) N1 [critical] Single dataset severely limits external validity of the core methodological claim. -  All experiments use only HarmThoughts. The paper positions Contribution 1 as \\\"follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts\\\" (Section 5) but provides no second-dataset validation. The cosine distance geometric argument (Appendix C) is computed on HarmThoughts traces only. HarmThoughts' right-skewed CP distribution and specific category composition may drive the observed patterns. Claim scope substantially exceeds evidence scope. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. This is the highest-priority issue. -  Action: Validate Table 2 core pattern on at least one additional safety benchmark, or significantly narrow generalizability claims. N2 [critical] Missing inter-annotator agreement (IAA) undermines CP annotation quality foundation. -  Section 7 states: \\\"Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations.\\\" The entire experimental framework depends on CP annotation quality. QwQ-32B's HS lead time of -1.2 to +1.9 steps falls within plausible annotation error. Appendix J's perturbation analysis tests random noise but cannot capture systematic annotation bias (consistently early/late CP placement). -  R1: Must-Fix. R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J provides indirect evidence). Director resolution:  Must-Fix  — CP is the paper's foundational concept; marginal 32B results are vulnerable to annotation uncertainty within noise range. -  Action: Report IAA if obtainable; conduct small-scale re-annotation study; or discuss expected noise magnitude based on annotation methodology. N3 [critical] Tiny 32B sample size (n=19-20) bears excessive inferential load. -  MDE at 80% power is 8.6-9.5pp (Table 35), far exceeding R1-32B's residualized +1.9pp. With W=5, probe input is 25,600-dimensional with only ~ 60 training samples — overfitting risk is severe even with L2 regularization. Despite correct † markers, the paper draws multiple substantive conclusions from these micro-samples: QwQ-32B \\\"position-competitive,\\\" R1-32B \\\"position-mediated,\\\" 32B cross-model divergence analysis, quadratic position absorption. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. -  Action: Collect more 32B data; restrict 32B discussion to purely descriptive observations; or reposition as pilot study with explicit power requirements. Should-Fix (7 issues) W1 [warning] Position baseline threatens core HS advantage — 2/4 models absorbed or severely weakened. \\n QwQ-32B's HS advantage fully absorbed by quadratic position term (Table 26); R1-32B residualized gap +1.9pp (p=0.914). The paper labels quadratic control as \\\"sensitivity analysis, not primary specification\\\" (Section 5) without sufficient justification for why linear should be preferred. HS probes may simply be better position encoders than text probes rather than capturing genuine safety-relevant features. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W2 [warning] MiniLM-384d is a weak baseline — capacity mismatch makes HS advantage partly expected. \\n HS representations from 7-32B models (3584-5120d) compared against 384d/22M-parameter MiniLM. At 1024d BGE-large, only 1/4 models passes HB. E5-Mistral-7B (2/4 HB) shows monotonic gradient: stronger encoder → smaller HS advantage. PCA capacity matching preserves advantage but does not fully resolve quality differences. Abstract leads with \\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against the weakest baseline. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W3 [warning] HB correction scope may be insufficient. \\n HB covers 12 final tests (3 metrics × 4 models), but total researcher degrees of freedom include 5 metrics × 7 W values × 28-64 layers × multiple thresholds × 2 split ratios. Evidence gradient classification itself is a post-hoc decision. Nested CV addresses within-loop selection for 7-8B but not the full exploration space. [ R1: Should-Fix, R2: Minor — Director keeps at Should-Fix given cumulative exploration acknowledged in Section 7 ] W4 [warning] 3/4 models share Qwen architecture — limited architectural diversity. \\n Only R1-8B (Llama) provides cross-architecture evidence, and it happens to show the strongest HS advantage. R1-32B and QwQ-32B share Qwen-32B base but show qualitatively different probe characteristics — training method may matter more than architecture, but tested on only one architecture pair. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W5 [warning] CP definition creates conceptual tension with online detection motivation. \\n CP requires observing the full trace (\\\"the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases\\\"), yet the introduction frames the work toward \\\"production detectors.\\\" R1 flagged this as Should-Fix; R2 rebutted (standard probing paradigm). Director resolution: the probing setup is methodologically valid, but the introduction should more clearly distinguish retrospective classification from online detection. [ R1 raised, R2 partial rebut ] W6 [warning] FPR 11-50% inconsistent with deployment narrative. \\n 76.7% oracle gap (Appendix I) further highlights the performance ceiling. The paper acknowledges in Limitations that methods \\\"characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors,\\\" but this caveat should appear earlier. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W7 [warning] \\\"Confirmatory\\\" terminology potentially misleading. \\n R2 flagged as Must-Fix (no pre-registration); R1 rebutted as Should-Fix (paper defines it operationally in footnote 1 as \\\"nested CV validated,\\\" not in the pre-registration sense). Director resolution:  Should-Fix  — the paper does provide a clear operational definition, making this a terminology issue rather than a methodological flaw. Renaming to \\\"validated\\\" or \\\"cross-validated\\\" would resolve the concern without affecting any substantive conclusion. Reviewer Consensus Both reviewers independently agree on: -  [ AGREE]  N1 : Single dataset is the most critical limitation (both marked critical without seeing each other's initial review) -  [ AGREE]  N3 : 32B sample size inadequate for the inferential load placed on it -  [ AGREE]  W1 : Position confound is serious for 2/4 models -  [ AGREE]  W2 : Encoder capacity mismatch partially explains HS advantage -  [ AGREE]  W4 : Architecture diversity insufficient (3/4 Qwen) -  [ AGREE]  S1-S4 : All strengths confirmed by both reviewers -  [ AGREE ] Methodological contribution (Contribution 1) has lasting community value -  [ AGREE ] Paper should be reframed with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization Reviewer Divergence D1: Ensemble evaluation. -  R1: Ensemble claim is well-supported — the claim is metric-agnostic selection (vs. text: 4/4 significant, p≤0.002), not superiority over best single metric. [ REBUT ] -  R2: Ens-Best is not significant (p>0.27), incremental value indistinguishable from zero. [ REBUT to R1's S5 ] -  Director note: Both interpretations are valid. Ensemble provides practical metric-agnostic value but does not improve over oracle single-metric selection. The claim boundary should be stated more clearly. D2: IAA severity. -  R1: Must-Fix (CP annotation quality is foundational; marginal 32B results vulnerable) -  R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J perturbation provides indirect evidence; CP comes from original benchmark) -  Director resolution: Must-Fix (see N2 above). D3: CP definition circularity. -  R1: Should-Fix (conceptual tension with online detection) -  R2: Rebut (standard probing paradigm; paper is analysis not deployment) -  Director resolution: Should-Fix (W5 above), but acknowledges R2's point that the probing paradigm itself is standard. Questions for Authors 1.  If Table 2's metric-layer interaction pattern were tested on WildJailbreak or another safety dataset, would it persist? This is the single most important open question. 2.  Was the \\\"confirmatory / significant / exploratory\\\" evidence tier classification determined before or after seeing results? If post-hoc, how does this not constitute additional reporting degrees of freedom? 3.  For QwQ-32B, why should the linear position specification be preferred over quadratic? Can you rule out that HS probes are simply better position encoders rather than capturing genuine safety content? 4.  With W=5 and 5120d hidden states, probe input is 25,600-dimensional but training set is ~ 60 samples. How do you ensure L2 regularization is sufficient to prevent overfitting? 5.  Are results stable across different harmful categories within HarmThoughts? Could the HS advantage be driven by specific attack types? 6.  R1 correctly noted that R2 misattributed R1-32B's 3.0pp selectivity to R1-8B. Please confirm: R1-8B selectivity is 14.1pp/22.9pp (well above threshold), and R1-32B residualized selectivity is 3.0pp (below threshold)? Missing References 1.  Zou et al. (2024) , \\\"Improving Alignment and Robustness with Circuit Breakers\\\" — representation-level safety intervention, directly relevant to causal validation discussion 2.  Templeton et al. (2024) , \\\"Scaling Monosemanticity\\\" — large-scale feature extraction for understanding safety representations 3.  Gelman & Loken (2013) , \\\"The garden of forking paths\\\" — already in bib ( gelman2013garden ) but never cited; Section 5's \\\"researcher degree of freedom\\\" discussion should cite this foundational work 4.  Turner et al. (2023) , activation addition — relevant to activation steering for safety External Review (Stanford Agentic Reviewer) Status: Skipped.  External review request was sent but skipped by user (2026-05-19T17:01:27Z). No external review data available for this round. Visual Layout Check (非评分项) Status: Timeout  (815 seconds elapsed without completion). Visual review agent did not return results in time. Known visual issues from reviewer comments: -  Figure 1 (p.6) heat map too small — axis labels and metric names hard to read (R1 m2) -  Figure 1 not colorblind-friendly (R2 minor) -  Abstract information density excessive for first-time readers (R1 m1, R2 minor) Overall Assessment Score: 3.0 (Findings) \\n Confidence: 4/5 Scoring Rationale Contribution 1 (metric-induced layer selection bias)  is the paper's core strength: a methodologically sound finding with practical community value that both reviewers recognized as important. However, it is validated on only one dataset, preventing confidence in the generalization claim. Contribution 2 (HS vs. text empirical comparison)  is substantially weakened by multiple compounding limitations: -  Single dataset (no external validity) -  32B results statistically underpowered (n=19-20, effects below MDE) -  Position confound absorbs HS advantage for 2/4 models -  Encoder-conditional advantage (1/4 HB at 1024d BGE-large) -  Missing IAA for CP annotations The paper's exceptional statistical rigor, honest limitations disclosure, and systematic negative results elevate it above typical Findings-level work in methodology. However, when half the results require † markers, footnotes, and multi-layer qualifications, the empirical signal is too fragile for Conference acceptance. Findings recommendation : Reframe with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization on one benchmark. The methodological warning about layer selection bias has sufficient community value for Findings. Conference acceptance would require : (a) second dataset validating layer selection bias generalizability, (b) larger 32B samples or withdrawal of 32B inferential claims, (c) stronger position control justification. Individual Reviewer Scores The 0.5 gap stems from: (1) R1 gives more weight to Contribution 1's independent methodological value; (2) R2 penalizes \\\"confirmatory\\\" terminology more severely; (3) R2 rates excitement lower (2.5/5 vs R1's 3.0/5). On substantive weaknesses (N1, N3, W1, W2, W4), both reviewers are in close agreement. Director's consolidated score follows R2's assessment:  3.0 (Findings) . Detailed Comments Abstract -  Information density too high — reads as compressed results summary rather than accessible abstract. Should present core finding first, then add qualifications incrementally. [ R1, R2 ] -  Leads with \\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against MiniLM-384d. Encoder-quality boundary should be reflected in abstract. [ R1, R2 ] Section 1 (Introduction) -  Problem framing is clear: methodological question (layer selection sensitivity) before empirical question (HS vs. text) -  \\\"Before building production detectors\\\" creates deployment expectations inconsistent with 11-50% FPR reality. [ R1 ] -  Contribution 2 listing reads defensively — too many qualifications in the contribution statement itself. [ R1 ] -  \\\"We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusions\\\" — R2 flagged as over-strong; R1 defended with Table 2 evidence. Director: the claim is supported but \\\"a study's\\\" is ambiguous (could imply published work was reversed). Section 2 (Related Work) -  Comprehensive and well-organized. Correct distinction between attack characterization / detection / mitigation. [ R1 ] -  Chan & Yong (2025) relationship discussed appropriately. [ R1 ] -  \\\"Alternative Detection Approaches\\\" paragraph could better distinguish temporal/spatial/cross-model methods. [ R2 ] Section 3 (Methods) -  CP definition (Section 3.1) is concise but oscillation handling needs more discussion. [ R1 ] -  MiniLM baseline weakness should be declared earlier. [ R1 ] -  Five-metric design (Section 3.3) is well-conceived — the methodological core. [ R1 ] -  Bootstrap clustering and HB implementation are correct. [ R1 ] Section 5 (Discussion) -  Correct distinction between Contribution 1 (geometric, potentially generalizable) and Contribution 2 (dataset-dependent). [ R1, R2 ] -  \\\"Researcher degree of freedom\\\" discussion should cite Gelman & Loken (2013) — already in bib but uncited. [ R1, R2 ] -  Ensemble proof-of-concept adds practical value; claim boundaries should be stated more precisely. [ R1, R2 diverge on evaluation ] Section 6 (Conclusion) -  Concise and appropriately conditional. [ R1 ] Section 7 (Limitations) -  Exceptionally comprehensive: dataset, statistical power, hyperparameters, architecture diversity, CP definition. [ R1, R2 ] -  \\\"One of the most honest limitations sections\\\" (R1) — but the volume of limitations is itself a signal about empirical fragility. Appendix (17 pages) -  Extensive robustness checks far exceeding typical NLP papers. [ R1 ] -  Bug disclosure (Appendix N) is excellent scientific practice.
