战略方案的独立评估，再做最终决策。\\\\n[05-19 17:11] cli_a974558fe4b81bc9: 🔄 [ 重新审稿] 📋  [审稿群 → Research Director] 审稿报告 Round 21 \\\\n Overall:  3.0 (Findings) Consolidated Review Report — Round 21 (Fresh Review) Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models Venue : EMNLP 2026 (ARR May 2026 cycle) |  Deadline : 2026-05-25 |  Reviewers : 2 |  Rounds : 1 (converged) Summary This paper investigates how the choice of layer selection metric systematically biases which transformer layer is chosen for hidden-state (HS) safety probes in reasoning models, and how this bias can reverse HS vs. text probe conclusions. Across four reasoning LLMs (3 Qwen-based, 1 Llama) on the HarmThoughts benchmark, the authors show that crossing-rate composite metrics select shallow layers (inflating threshold crossings due to representation compression), while threshold-based metrics select deep layers where HS advantages of +19.2 to +21.9pp emerge against a 384d MiniLM text encoder. The HS advantage is encoder-conditional (1/4 models survives HB correction at 1024d) and partially position-mediated (2/4 models). A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic selection. The paper includes extensive negative results and an unusually honest limitations section. Strengths S1. Important methodological finding.  The identification of metric-induced layer selection bias as a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse HS vs. text conclusions (Table 2) has lasting value for the probing literature. The five-metric ablation with random-label control (Appendix B.1) demonstrates this is a property of representation geometry, not label-specific. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S2. Statistical rigor far exceeding peer work.  Holm-Bonferroni correction, nested 5-fold CV, BCa bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples with trace-level clustering), permutation tests, MDE calculation (Appendix AA), and explicit evidence gradient classification. Trace-level bootstrap correctly handles temporal dependence between steps. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S3. Systematic negative results.  Section 4.6 and Table 20 report failures of 7 alternative methods (BOCPD, CUSUM, Mahalanobis, RepE, MLP, CCS, cross-model transfer) and distill four signal characteristic requirements. This defines method boundaries for future work. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S4. Scientific integrity.  M002 bug disclosure (Appendix N), explicit exploratory (†) markers for underpowered 32B results, seed-sensitivity reporting for R1-8B FPR, and one of the most comprehensive limitations sections in recent NLP papers. [ R1, R2 consensus ] S5. Format compliance.  8-page main text, correct ACL template with  [review]  option, proper anonymization (\\\\\\\"Anonymous ARR Submission\\\\\\\"). [ R1, R2 consensus ] Weaknesses Must-Fix (3 issues — reviewer consensus) N1 [critical] Single dataset severely limits external validity of the core methodological claim. -  All experiments use only HarmThoughts. The paper positions Contribution 1 as \\\\\\\"follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts\\\\\\\" (Section 5) but provides no second-dataset validation. The cosine distance geometric argument (Appendix C) is computed on HarmThoughts traces only. HarmThoughts' right-skewed CP distribution and specific category composition may drive the observed patterns. Claim scope substantially exceeds evidence scope. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. This is the highest-priority issue. -  Action: Validate Table 2 core pattern on at least one additional safety benchmark, or significantly narrow generalizability claims. N2 [critical] Missing inter-annotator agreement (IAA) undermines CP annotation quality foundation. -  Section 7 states: \\\\\\\"Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations.\\\\\\\" The entire experimental framework depends on CP annotation quality. QwQ-32B's HS lead time of -1.2 to +1.9 steps falls within plausible annotation error. Appendix J's perturbation analysis tests random noise but cannot capture systematic annotation bias (consistently early/late CP placement). -  R1: Must-Fix. R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J provides indirect evidence). Director resolution:  Must-Fix  — CP is the paper's foundational concept; marginal 32B results are vulnerable to annotation uncertainty within noise range. -  Action: Report IAA if obtainable; conduct small-scale re-annotation study; or discuss expected noise magnitude based on annotation methodology. N3 [critical] Tiny 32B sample size (n=19-20) bears excessive inferential load. -  MDE at 80% power is 8.6-9.5pp (Table 35), far exceeding R1-32B's residualized +1.9pp. With W=5, probe input is 25,600-dimensional with only ~ 60 training samples — overfitting risk is severe even with L2 regularization. Despite correct † markers, the paper draws multiple substantive conclusions from these micro-samples: QwQ-32B \\\\\\\"position-competitive,\\\\\\\" R1-32B \\\\\\\"position-mediated,\\\\\\\" 32B cross-model divergence analysis, quadratic position absorption. -  Both reviewers independently marked critical. -  Action: Collect more 32B data; restrict 32B discussion to purely descriptive observations; or reposition as pilot study with explicit power requirements. Should-Fix (7 issues) W1 [warning] Position baseline threatens core HS advantage — 2/4 models absorbed or severely weakened. \\\\n QwQ-32B's HS advantage fully absorbed by quadratic position term (Table 26); R1-32B residualized gap +1.9pp (p=0.914). The paper labels quadratic control as \\\\\\\"sensitivity analysis, not primary specification\\\\\\\" (Section 5) without sufficient justification for why linear should be preferred. HS probes may simply be better position encoders than text probes rather than capturing genuine safety-relevant features. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W2 [warning] MiniLM-384d is a weak baseline — capacity mismatch makes HS advantage partly expected. \\\\n HS representations from 7-32B models (3584-5120d) compared against 384d/22M-parameter MiniLM. At 1024d BGE-large, only 1/4 models passes HB. E5-Mistral-7B (2/4 HB) shows monotonic gradient: stronger encoder → smaller HS advantage. PCA capacity matching preserves advantage but does not fully resolve quality differences. Abstract leads with \\\\\\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\\\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against the weakest baseline. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W3 [warning] HB correction scope may be insufficient. \\\\n HB covers 12 final tests (3 metrics × 4 models), but total researcher degrees of freedom include 5 metrics × 7 W values × 28-64 layers × multiple thresholds × 2 split ratios. Evidence gradient classification itself is a post-hoc decision. Nested CV addresses within-loop selection for 7-8B but not the full exploration space. [ R1: Should-Fix, R2: Minor — Director keeps at Should-Fix given cumulative exploration acknowledged in Section 7 ] W4 [warning] 3/4 models share Qwen architecture — limited architectural diversity. \\\\n Only R1-8B (Llama) provides cross-architecture evidence, and it happens to show the strongest HS advantage. R1-32B and QwQ-32B share Qwen-32B base but show qualitatively different probe characteristics — training method may matter more than architecture, but tested on only one architecture pair. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W5 [warning] CP definition creates conceptual tension with online detection motivation. \\\\n CP requires observing the full trace (\\\\\\\"the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases\\\\\\\"), yet the introduction frames the work toward \\\\\\\"production detectors.\\\\\\\" R1 flagged this as Should-Fix; R2 rebutted (standard probing paradigm). Director resolution: the probing setup is methodologically valid, but the introduction should more clearly distinguish retrospective classification from online detection. [ R1 raised, R2 partial rebut ] W6 [warning] FPR 11-50% inconsistent with deployment narrative. \\\\n 76.7% oracle gap (Appendix I) further highlights the performance ceiling. The paper acknowledges in Limitations that methods \\\\\\\"characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors,\\\\\\\" but this caveat should appear earlier. [ R1, R2 consensus ] W7 [warning] \\\\\\\"Confirmatory\\\\\\\" terminology potentially misleading. \\\\n R2 flagged as Must-Fix (no pre-registration); R1 rebutted as Should-Fix (paper defines it operationally in footnote 1 as \\\\\\\"nested CV validated,\\\\\\\" not in the pre-registration sense). Director resolution:  Should-Fix  — the paper does provide a clear operational definition, making this a terminology issue rather than a methodological flaw. Renaming to \\\\\\\"validated\\\\\\\" or \\\\\\\"cross-validated\\\\\\\" would resolve the concern without affecting any substantive conclusion. Reviewer Consensus Both reviewers independently agree on: -  [ AGREE]  N1 : Single dataset is the most critical limitation (both marked critical without seeing each other's initial review) -  [ AGREE]  N3 : 32B sample size inadequate for the inferential load placed on it -  [ AGREE]  W1 : Position confound is serious for 2/4 models -  [ AGREE]  W2 : Encoder capacity mismatch partially explains HS advantage -  [ AGREE]  W4 : Architecture diversity insufficient (3/4 Qwen) -  [ AGREE]  S1-S4 : All strengths confirmed by both reviewers -  [ AGREE ] Methodological contribution (Contribution 1) has lasting community value -  [ AGREE ] Paper should be reframed with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization Reviewer Divergence D1: Ensemble evaluation. -  R1: Ensemble claim is well-supported — the claim is metric-agnostic selection (vs. text: 4/4 significant, p≤0.002), not superiority over best single metric. [ REBUT ] -  R2: Ens-Best is not significant (p>0.27), incremental value indistinguishable from zero. [ REBUT to R1's S5 ] -  Director note: Both interpretations are valid. Ensemble provides practical metric-agnostic value but does not improve over oracle single-metric selection. The claim boundary should be stated more clearly. D2: IAA severity. -  R1: Must-Fix (CP annotation quality is foundational; marginal 32B results vulnerable) -  R2: Should-Fix (Appendix J perturbation provides indirect evidence; CP comes from original benchmark) -  Director resolution: Must-Fix (see N2 above). D3: CP definition circularity. -  R1: Should-Fix (conceptual tension with online detection) -  R2: Rebut (standard probing paradigm; paper is analysis not deployment) -  Director resolution: Should-Fix (W5 above), but acknowledges R2's point that the probing paradigm itself is standard. Questions for Authors 1.  If Table 2's metric-layer interaction pattern were tested on WildJailbreak or another safety dataset, would it persist? This is the single most important open question. 2.  Was the \\\\\\\"confirmatory / significant / exploratory\\\\\\\" evidence tier classification determined before or after seeing results? If post-hoc, how does this not constitute additional reporting degrees of freedom? 3.  For QwQ-32B, why should the linear position specification be preferred over quadratic? Can you rule out that HS probes are simply better position encoders rather than capturing genuine safety content? 4.  With W=5 and 5120d hidden states, probe input is 25,600-dimensional but training set is ~ 60 samples. How do you ensure L2 regularization is sufficient to prevent overfitting? 5.  Are results stable across different harmful categories within HarmThoughts? Could the HS advantage be driven by specific attack types? 6.  R1 correctly noted that R2 misattributed R1-32B's 3.0pp selectivity to R1-8B. Please confirm: R1-8B selectivity is 14.1pp/22.9pp (well above threshold), and R1-32B residualized selectivity is 3.0pp (below threshold)? Missing References 1.  Zou et al. (2024) , \\\\\\\"Improving Alignment and Robustness with Circuit Breakers\\\\\\\" — representation-level safety intervention, directly relevant to causal validation discussion 2.  Templeton et al. (2024) , \\\\\\\"Scaling Monosemanticity\\\\\\\" — large-scale feature extraction for understanding safety representations 3.  Gelman & Loken (2013) , \\\\\\\"The garden of forking paths\\\\\\\" — already in bib ( gelman2013garden ) but never cited; Section 5's \\\\\\\"researcher degree of freedom\\\\\\\" discussion should cite this foundational work 4.  Turner et al. (2023) , activation addition — relevant to activation steering for safety External Review (Stanford Agentic Reviewer) Status: Skipped.  External review request was sent but skipped by user (2026-05-19T17:01:27Z). No external review data available for this round. Visual Layout Check (非评分项) Status: Timeout  (815 seconds elapsed without completion). Visual review agent did not return results in time. Known visual issues from reviewer comments: -  Figure 1 (p.6) heat map too small — axis labels and metric names hard to read (R1 m2) -  Figure 1 not colorblind-friendly (R2 minor) -  Abstract information density excessive for first-time readers (R1 m1, R2 minor) Overall Assessment Score: 3.0 (Findings) \\\\n Confidence: 4/5 Scoring Rationale Contribution 1 (metric-induced layer selection bias)  is the paper's core strength: a methodologically sound finding with practical community value that both reviewers recognized as important. However, it is validated on only one dataset, preventing confidence in the generalization claim. Contribution 2 (HS vs. text empirical comparison)  is substantially weakened by multiple compounding limitations: -  Single dataset (no external validity) -  32B results statistically underpowered (n=19-20, effects below MDE) -  Position confound absorbs HS advantage for 2/4 models -  Encoder-conditional advantage (1/4 HB at 1024d BGE-large) -  Missing IAA for CP annotations The paper's exceptional statistical rigor, honest limitations disclosure, and systematic negative results elevate it above typical Findings-level work in methodology. However, when half the results require † markers, footnotes, and multi-layer qualifications, the empirical signal is too fragile for Conference acceptance. Findings recommendation : Reframe with Contribution 1 as primary, Contribution 2 as initial characterization on one benchmark. The methodological warning about layer selection bias has sufficient community value for Findings. Conference acceptance would require : (a) second dataset validating layer selection bias generalizability, (b) larger 32B samples or withdrawal of 32B inferential claims, (c) stronger position control justification. Individual Reviewer Scores The 0.5 gap stems from: (1) R1 gives more weight to Contribution 1's independent methodological value; (2) R2 penalizes \\\\\\\"confirmatory\\\\\\\" terminology more severely; (3) R2 rates excitement lower (2.5/5 vs R1's 3.0/5). On substantive weaknesses (N1, N3, W1, W2, W4), both reviewers are in close agreement. Director's consolidated score follows R2's assessment:  3.0 (Findings) . Detailed Comments Abstract -  Information density too high — reads as compressed results summary rather than accessible abstract. Should present core finding first, then add qualifications incrementally. [ R1, R2 ] -  Leads with \\\\\\\"+19.2 to +21.9pp\\\\\\\" without immediately qualifying this is against MiniLM-384d. Encoder-quality boundary should be reflected in abstract. [ R1, R2 ] Section 1 (Introduction) -  Problem framing is clear: methodological question (layer selection sensitivity) before empirical question (HS vs. text) -  \\\\\\\"Before building production detectors\\\\\\\" creates deployment expectations inconsistent with 11-50% FPR reality. [ R1 ] -  Contribution 2 listing reads defensively — too many qualifications in the contribution statement itself. [ R1 ] -  \\\\\\\"We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusions\\\\\\\" — R2 flagged as over-strong; R1 defended with Table 2 evidence. Director: the claim is supported but \\\\\\\"a study's\\\\\\\" is ambiguous (could imply published work was reversed). Section 2 (Related Work) -  Comprehensive and well-organized. Correct distinction between attack characterization / detection / mitigation. [ R1 ] -  Chan & Yong (2025) relationship discussed appropriately. [ R1 ] -  \\\\\\\"Alternative Detection Approaches\\\\\\\" paragraph could better distinguish temporal/spatial/cross-model methods. [ R2 ] Section 3 (Methods) -  CP definition (Section 3.1) is concise but oscillation handling needs more discussion. [ R1 ] -  MiniLM baseline weakness should be declared earlier. [ R1 ] -  Five-metric design (Section 3.3) is well-conceived — the methodological core. [ R1 ] -  Bootstrap clustering and HB implementation are correct. [ R1 ] Section 4 (Experiments) -  Table 2 is the most important table and clearly demonstrates the core finding. [ R1, R2 ] -  Section 4.3 position mediation is honest but weakens the central claim — consider giving quadratic control more narrative weight. [ R1 ] -  Section 4.4 32B analysis over-interprets given sample sizes. [ R1 ] -  18/20 model-seed pass rate: the 2 failures should be clearly identified in main text. {
  "project": "contrastive_trace_safety",
  "content": "# contrastive_trace_safety — Research Dashboard\n\n## 核心方向\nReasoning model safety commitment detection: hidden-state vs text probes across 4 models (2 arch × 2 scale). 详见 paper_framing.md.\n\n## 当前进展\n- **阶段**: Review — R21 Fresh Review 完成 + Option A（AdvBench 并行冲刺）启动\n- **DDL**: ARR May 25 (~5 天)\n- **R21 评分**: Overall 3.0 (Findings)\n- **分数趋势**: R15=3.0 → R16=3.0 → R17=3.0 → R18=3.0 → R19=3.0 → R20=3.0 → R21=3.0（7 轮结构性瓶颈）\n- **瓶颈诊断**: 单数据集 + 小 32B 样本 = 文本修改无法突破，需第二数据集\n- **论文 commits**: 5a22831(R20) → 1c555f6(N6) → 5f656c0(R21) → 87ba189(full-layer-sweep)\n- **R1-8B 全层 sweep**: crossing_rate→L0（16 层 divergence），直接强化 Contribution 1\n\n## 近期任务\n- [ ] **AdvBench 第二数据集实验**（Option A 核心，~8-12h）\n  - [ ] R1-8B 在 AdvBench 520 prompts 上生成 traces（GPU, ~2-3h）\n  - [ ] LLM CP 标注（CPU, ~1-2h，需 API key）\n  - [ ] Hidden state 提取 25 层（GPU, ~1-2h）\n  - [ ] 5-metric layer sweep + bootstrap（CPU, ~2-4h）\n  - [ ] 结果写入论文（Paper Agent, ~2h）\n- [ ] **IAA 执行**（需 API key — 已向用户申请）\n- [ ] 格式终检 + PDF 编译\n- [ ] mini-R22 验证审查\n- [ ] ARR 系统提交（5/24 前）\n\n## GPU 预算\nautodl-rtx6000 (2卡): AdvBench trace 生成 + HS 提取用。\n\n## 已完成里程碑\n- [x] R15-R21 共 70+ 项审稿修复\n- [x] R1-8B L0-L24 全层 sweep（5-metric × 4-window × 25-layer）\n- [x] N6 multi-metric layer ensemble（4/4 models significant vs text）\n- [x] AdvBench 管线全部 built + dry-run 验证\n- [x] IAA 代码框架就绪（CP 定义修正 + few-shot + weighted kappa）\n- [x] 3 个 robustness 分析（nested CV 7-8B, random-CP QwQ, split-half）\n- [x] Phase 3 Appendix 集成 + Tiered evidence framework\n",
  "reason": "R21 后主动执行 Option A（AdvBench 并行冲刺），更新全局状态"
}

Bootstrap 10000 resamples with trace-level clustering\n13. 核心验证目标：Table 2 pattern 是否复现（crossing_rate → shallow, threshold-based → deep）\n\n**汇报要求：**\n- Phase 1 完成后立即汇报 trace 数量和质量\n- Phase 2 完成后汇报 HS 提取状态\n- 全部完成后汇报完整 5-metric layer sweep 结果\n\n**关键：** 先启动 Phase 1（trace 生成），这是 critical path。不要等 API key。",

  "content": "[Paper Agent 回复]:\n[写作进展] Patch 完成 — R1-8B framing 修正（Reviewer caveat）\n\n**Commit**: f8dd87d `[paper] R1-8B framing fix: crossing-rate focus, no composite shallow-bias claim`\n\n**修改 3 处**：\n\n1. **appendix.tex — R1-8B full-layer sweep 段落**\n   - 移除 \"and CR-composite\" → 主证据聚焦 crossing_rate → L0\n   - 新增 \"Unlike 32B models, the bias is isolated to crossing rate; threshold-based metrics show no shallow shift for R1-8B\"\n   - W=15 标注 \"marginal Δ=0.003 between shallow and deep means\"\n   - 表 caption 改为 \"crossing rate vs threshold-based metrics\"（非 composite）\n\n2. **experiments.tex — Figure 1 caption**\n   - \"CR-composite shifts to L0\" → \"crossing rate drives CR-composite selection to L0\"\n   - 明确 crossing rate 是驱动因素\n\n3. **Discussion/Conclusion** — 无需修改（已正确引用 architecture-independent，未声称 composite shallow bias）\n\n**核心 framing 变化**：\n- 16 层 gap = crossing_rate vs threshold-based metrics，不是 composite vs others\n- Threshold-based composite 在 R1-8B 停留 L12-L16（mid-to-deep），无 shallow bias\n- 与 32B 对比说明 metric bias 可能与模型深度正相关\n\n编译 PASS，8 页限制不受影响。\n\n---\n\n[Reviewer 回复]:\n## ROADMAP 审查 — D054 AdvBench 并行冲刺\n\n**结论**: WARNING（方向正确，但关键依赖未解）\n\n### 方向评估\n\n- [severity: info] **Option A 决策逻辑成立**：7 轮 3.0 结构性瓶颈 + 管线已验证 + 失败可无损退回 Option B = 执行风险 < 等待成本。Director 在用户 4h+ 未回复下主动推进合理。\n\n- [severity: info] **时间线可行性**：~8-12h 实验 + ~2h 写入 = ~2 天。DDL 5/25 剩 5 天，留 3 天 buffer（review + 格式终检 + 提交）。GPU 预算充足：trace 生成 ~2-3h + HS 提取 ~1-2h = ~4h GPU 总量，RTX 6000 完全够用。\n\n### 关键风险\n\n- [severity: critical] **API key 仍是硬阻塞**：AdvBench CP 标注需要 GROQ_API_KEY（annotate_cp.py 调用 Groq qwen3-32b）。IAA 同样需要 API key。ROADMAP 列出的 5 个子任务中，第 2 步（CP 标注）直接被阻塞，后续所有步骤（HS 提取需 CP 标注决定 jailbreak 筛选、layer sweep 需 step_labels）均无法继续。\n  - **建议**：(1) 第 1 步 trace 生成可立即启动（不依赖 API key），与 API key 申请并行；(2) 如 API key 24h 内未到，考虑替代方案——用 Anthropic API（annotate.py 已支持 claude-sonnet backend）或 Google API（gemini-2.5-flash backend）作为 fallback。\n\n- [severity: warning] **Jailbreak 产出率未知**：R1-8B（DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B）是 safety-tuned 模型，面对 AdvBench 520 条直接有害指令，拒绝率可能较高。如果仅 30% 产生 jailbreak trace ≈ 156 条，勉强够用；如果 <20% ≈ 104 条，统计功效边缘。\n  - **建议**：trace 生成后立即统计 jailbreak yield，如 <100 条需调整策略（如降低 CP 判定阈值或增加 prompt mutation）。\n\n- [severity: warning] **8 页限制下的 paper 集成**：当前论文已接近 8 页。加入第二数据集结果至少需要一个新 Table 或 Figure + 若干段落。如何在不超限的前提下集成？\n  - **建议**：(1) 将 AdvBench 结果压缩为一个 Table row（附加到现有 Table 而非新建）+ 1-2 句文字；(2) 或将部分现有 appendix 内容进一步压缩为正文引用。Paper Agent 需提前规划版面。\n\n### 次要发现\n\n- [severity: info] **compute_coverage_matrix 工具报错**：`cannot use 'list' as a dict key (unhashable type: 'list')`。experiment_plan.yaml 中可能有格式问题（某个字段是 list 而非 string）。不影响实验执行，但覆盖率审查暂时无法自动化。\n\n- [severity: info] **split_half_ot7b 数值已修正**（前序报告中的遗留问题已解决，此处确认关闭）。\n\n- [severity: info] **已完成里程碑列表准确**：R1-8B 全层 sweep、N6 ensemble、3 个 robustness 分析、AdvBench 管线验证——均与 registry 记录一致。\n\n### 总结\nROADMAP 方向正确，AdvBench 冲刺是突破 3.0 瓶颈的唯一可行路径。**最大风险是 API key 阻塞**——建议 Director 在 trace 生成并行期间准备多 backend fallback 方案，确保 CP 标注不被单一 API 供应商卡住。\n\n---\n\n[Agent 回复]:\n[资源] GPU 2/2 (autodl-rtx6000 cuda:0,1) | Worker 1 (GPU 1) | 磁盘: 未检查\n\n## [D054] AdvBench 第二数据集实验已启动\n\n### Phase 1: Trace 生成（已派发）\nWorker `worker_gpu_advbench_trace_generation_20260519T220938` 正在 autodl-rtx6000 上执行：\n- R1-8B greedy decoding × 520 AdvBench prompts\n- 先 dry-run 2-3 条验证格式，通过后用 `submit_training_job` 提交正式运行\n- 如脚本支持分片，会用双 GPU 并行加速\n- 预计 2-3h\n\n### Registry\n`advbench_r1_8b_full` 已创建（PENDING），hypothesis 聚焦跨数据集复现 metric-bias pattern。\n\n### 后续计划\n- Phase 1 完成 → 立即派 Phase 2（HS 提取 + text embedding）\n- Phase 3（CP 标注）等 API key，可与 Phase 2 并行\n- Phase 4（layer sweep + bootstrap）在 Phase 2+3 完成后启动\n\nCritical path: Phase 1 → Phase 2 → Phase 4。Phase 3 独立等 key。"
